{"rating": 4.0, "title": "Convenient Means to Making Ice", "text": "We have issues with our ice maker in our refrigerator. It is far too slow to produce ice and we have considered getting another refrigerator because of it. We usually just buy bags of ice to solve the problem, but for convenience sake, I decided to get this Frigidaire ice maker.<br /><br />Compact in size, this ice maker sits on the countertop and produces just enough ice to make it useful for beverages and other modest needs. You just open the flip top, pour in water, and wait about 10 to 15 minutes for the first batch of cubes of ice to be made. The small cubes are bell/gumdrop shaped and are small enough to fit in tight spaces, making them good for those who want to add ice to, say, water bottles and other receptacles that cannot handle normal sized ice cubes.<br /><br />The outside of this ice maker has lights that indicate Ice Full, Add Water, and Ice Making. The top two indicators display a red light, to get your attention The one annoyance I have with this ice maker is that the ice tends to stack up in the back, which triggers the sensor and causes the \u2018Ice Full\u2019 light to come on. There could, in fact, be plenty of space in the ice bin and you will need to open, pull the ice down from the back, and close to make the ice maker resume. The problem is that the ice is dumped in back and stacks up on the back edge.
